1	# Notes
2	
3	See [design.md](../crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md)
4	
5	
6	## jj tips
7	
8	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
9	
10	```
11	## Chores format
12	
13	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
14	example: chores-01.md
15	
16	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
17	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
18	
19	Example chore file:
20	```
21	# Chores-01.md
22	 
23	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
24	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
25	how and why of a particular chore.
26	
27	## Do something (1.3.1)
28	
29	Describe something
30	```
31	
32	## Versioning during development
33	
34	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
35	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
36	we go.
37	
38	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
39	
40	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
41	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
42	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
43	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
44	
45	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
46	
47	For multi-step:
48	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
49	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
50	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
51	
52	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
53	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
54	commits were exploratory vs final.
55	
56	## Todo format
57	
58	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
59	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
60	references.
61	
62	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
63	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
64	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
65	
66	Examples:
67	
68	# Todo
69	
70	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
71	- Fix bug Y [1]
72	
73	# Done
74	
75	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
76	
77	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
78	
```

33	- Add `--repeat N` flag to goal1/goal2 to run the full

40	- Revisit band-table `range` semantics — drop the `+1` so
41	  `first == last → range = 0` (true "no spread" instead of
42	  today's "1 tk / rounds to 0 ns"). `last - first` reads
43	  more naturally and matches what a reader expects "range"
44	  to mean. Pre-existing behavior, not introduced in 0.3.0-3
45	- **Bound `TProbe`'s records-buffer memory.** Each scope
46	  appends a 32-byte `Record` to an unbounded `Vec`; at
47	  `goal1 --inner 10` (~13M scopes/s), 20 s of measurement
48	  buffers 260M records ≈ 8 GB minimum, ~16 GB after
49	  `Vec` power-of-2 over-allocation, which swaps/OOMs on
50	  typical dev boxes. Pre-existing from 0.1.0-2 when the
51	  probe was vendored; becomes hard to miss at long
52	  measurement windows + small `inner`. Options (pick one
53	  once we instrument drain cost):
54	  - (a) **Hard cap + truncation counter.** Fixed max
55	    records (e.g. 64M ≈ 2 GB); past the cap, increment a
56	    dropped counter and skip the `push`. Report shows
57	    `truncated: N scopes dropped`. Preserves hot-path
58	    cost. Partial data on very long runs.
59	  - (b) **Periodic inline drain.** When records hit a
60	    threshold, drain into the histogram and continue.
61	    Memory bounded. Adds occasional drain stalls (ms-ish)
62	    that show up in the `p99-max` tail.
63	  - (c) **Inline drain, no buffer.** `end` / `end_batch`
64	    compute `per_event` and call `hist.record` directly.
65	    Fully bounded memory, no stalls, but adds ~15–20 ns
66	    to the hot path — non-trivial when the probe is
67	    measuring ~5 ns work per event.
68	  - (d) **Skinnier record (just `u64` per-event ticks).**
69	    4× memory reduction. Buys time but doesn't fix the
70	    unbounded-growth shape.
71	- Clean up `SingleThreadRuntime::run_for` inner loop:
72	  (a) remove the unnecessary `let Self { actors, queue, probe } = self;`
73	      destructure — disjoint `&mut` through named field paths

1	# actor-x1
2	
3	An experiment in creating an actor model in rust
4	see [crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md](crates/actor-x1/notes/design.md)
5	for details.
6	
7	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
8	a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
9	is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
10	bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
11	is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
12	is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
13	
14	The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
15	which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
16	first test bed.
17	
18	## Cloning
19	
20	The Bot session repo is setup as a git submodule which
21	makes it easier to clone both repos. The easiest is to
22	clone both when doing the initial clone:
23	```
24	git clone --recurisve git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
25	```
26	
27	If you forget to use --recurisze you need to do two additional
28	steps "init" and "update":
29	```
30	git clone git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
31	git submodule init
32	git submodule update
33	```
34	
35	And these can be combined using 'update --init' if you like
36	```
37	git clone git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
38	git submodule update --init
39	```
40	
41	## Usage
42	
43	Stage 1 (the PoC landed at 0.1.0) provides two binaries:
44	
45	- **`goal1`** — two actors on one thread, ping-ponging a
46	  message through a shared `VecDeque`.
47	- **`goal2`** — two actors on two threads, ping-ponging over
48	  `std::sync::mpsc` channels.
49	
50	Install with `cargo install --path crates/actor-x1`; both share
51	a common CLI shape:
52	
53	```
54	$ goal1 0.5 -i 1000 -p 0
55	actor-x1 0.3.1
56	goal1: 102243000 messages in 0.500s (204.486 M msg/s, inner=1000)
57	  pinning: main → core 0
58	  apparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); loop_per_iter=0.93 tk (0.24 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 1 tk (0.26 ns)
59	
60	  tprobe: goal1.dispatch [count=102,243]
61	                first       last      range        count   mean      adj mean
62	    min-p1        0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
63	    p1-p10        0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
64	    p10-p20       4.5 ns     4.5 ns     0.3 ns    27,412    4.5 ns        4.2 ns
65	    p20-p30       0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
66	    p30-p40       4.7 ns     4.7 ns     0.3 ns    22,761    4.7 ns        4.5 ns
67	    p40-p50       0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
68	    p50-p60       0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
69	    p60-p70       0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
70	    p70-p80       5.0 ns     5.0 ns     0.3 ns    49,494    5.0 ns        4.7 ns
71	    p80-p90       0.0 ns     0.0 ns     0.0 ns         0    0.0 ns        0.0 ns
72	    p90-p99       5.3 ns     5.8 ns     0.8 ns     1,722    5.6 ns        5.4 ns
73	    p99-max       6.1 ns    49.0 ns    43.2 ns       854    8.5 ns        8.3 ns
74	    mean                                                    4.8 ns        4.6 ns
75	    stdev                                                   0.5 ns
76	    mean min-p99                                            4.8 ns        4.6 ns
77	    stdev min-p99                                           0.2 ns
78	```
79	
80	### Flags
81	
82	- **`<duration_s>`** — positional, required; measurement window
83	  in seconds.
84	- **`-w` / `--warmup <SECS>`** — default 0.5; run the same
85	  dispatch loop with the probe active for this long, then clear
86	  the probe at the boundary so only steady-state data reaches
87	  the report. Set to 0 for quickest iteration; raise to 10 s or
88	  more for reference measurements where you want the CPU fully
89	  settled.
90	- **`-i` / `--inner <N>`** *(goal1 only)* — default 1, ≥1;
91	  dispatches per probe scope. Larger values amortize the probe's
92	  two-`rdtsc` apparatus cost and give the histogram meaningful
93	  dynamic range. goal2 fixes `inner=1` because its per-channel
94	  queue holds at most one message.
95	- **`-t` / `--ticks`** — display probe values as raw ticks
96	  instead of nanoseconds.
97	- **`-d` / `--decimals <N>`** — fractional precision of numeric
98	  cells in the band table. Omit for a mode-aware default
99	  (0 for ticks, 1 for ns) — typically what you want. Pass an
100	  integer N to force N decimals in either mode (e.g. `-d 2`
101	  for 2-decimal ns, `-d 0` for integer ns).
102	- **`-p` / `--pin <CORES>`** — pin threads to logical CPUs. goal1 uses
103	  the first core in the list; goal2 pins actor `i` to
104	  `cores[i % len]`. Accepts comma lists and ranges: `--pin 5`,
105	  `--pin 0,1`, `--pin 0-3`, `--pin 0,0` (oversubscribe).
106	
107	### Typical invocations
108	
109	```
110	# Short iterative smoke, no warmup:
111	goal1 0.1 -w 0 -i 1000
112	
113	# Full measurement with pinning:
114	goal1 2 -i 1000 -p 0
115	
116	# Goal2 with actors on separate cores (same complex):
117	goal2 1 -p 0,1
118	```
119	
120	## Reading the band table
121	
122	The `tprobe` block is a 12-row percentile histogram with four
123	summary rows. Every row renders every run, even when a band
124	holds no data — a zero row is distinguishable from a missing row.
125	
126	### Header
127	
128	```
129	tprobe: <name> [count=N]
130	```
131	
132	`<name>` identifies the probe (`goal1.dispatch`,
133	`goal2.dispatch.actor0`, …). `count=N` is the number of **scopes**
134	drained into the histogram, not total dispatches — at `--inner
135	1000` one scope covers 1,000 dispatches, so `count` here is
136	`(total_messages / inner)`. Total dispatch count is in the summary
137	line above.
138	
139	### Rows
140	
141	Twelve percentile bands in order: `min-p1`, `p1-p10`, …, `p90-p99`,
142	`p99-max`. Each row covers the samples whose rank in the sorted
143	distribution falls within that percentile range.
144	
145	### Columns
146	
147	- **first** — lowest value observed in the band.
148	- **last** — highest value observed in the band.
149	- **range** — `last - first + 1`; width of the band's value range.
150	- **count** — number of samples in the band.
151	- **mean** — average value within the band. Raw, unadjusted —
152	  same hardware-tick deltas the probe recorded.
153	- **adj mean** — same mean, minus the per-event apparatus
154	  correction from calibration (clamped at 0). See the
155	  `apparatus:` line for the correction amount and
156	  [`crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`](crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md)
157	  for the formal model.
158	
159	### Summary rows
160	
161	- **mean / stdev** — whole-histogram statistics, including the
162	  `p99-max` tail. `adj mean` on the `mean` row applies the
163	  per-event correction the same way as the band rows; `stdev`
164	  leaves the `adj mean` column blank because standard deviation
165	  is shift-invariant.
166	- **mean min-p99 / stdev min-p99** — same statistics excluding
167	  `p99-max`. Useful for A/B-ing code changes because the tail can
168	  be dominated by rare outliers (OS preemption, lock contention,
169	  interrupts) that drown signal in the body of the distribution.
170	  Same raw-vs-adj treatment as the `mean` / `stdev` pair.
171	
172	### Why a band can be all zeros
173	
174	The renderer walks `hdrhistogram` buckets and assigns each bucket
175	to one band by its cumulative mid-rank. A tight distribution has
176	most samples in a handful of buckets, and each bucket lands
177	entirely in one band — so neighboring bands show zeros even
178	though they'd nominally hold ~10% of the data. It's a display
179	artifact, not missing data: the samples are visible in the
180	adjacent non-zero band. A `stdev min-p99` near 0 alongside a
181	couple of dense bands is a strong signal that the underlying
182	distribution is spike-heavy.
183	
184	### The `apparatus:` line
185	
186	```
187	apparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); loop_per_iter=0.93 tk (0.24 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 1 tk (0.26 ns)
188	```
189	
190	Reports the fitted overhead model from calibration.
191	
192	- **`framing`** — hardware floor cost of the probe's two-`rdtsc`
193	  pair, paid once per scope. Measured as the intercept of a
194	  two-point fit on empty `black_box(1)` scopes.
195	- **`loop_per_iter`** — per-iteration cost of the calibration's
196	  empty inner loop (one `black_box(1)` per iter). Measured as
197	  the slope of the same fit.
198	- **`per-event at inner=N`** — the per-event correction shown in
199	  the band-table's `adj mean` column: `framing / N +
200	  loop_per_iter`, rounded to the nearest tick. Amortizes toward
201	  `loop_per_iter` as `inner` grows.
202	
203	Calibration runs unconditionally (~10 ms wall-clock); the
204	histogram still stores raw per-event values, and this correction
205	is applied only in the `adj mean` display column. See
206	[`crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md`](crates/tprobe/notes/overhead-model.md)
207	for the derivation and the unmeasurable-overhead category.
208	
209	### The `pinning:` line
210	
211	```
212	pinning: actor0→core0, actor1→core4
213	```
214	
215	Shows what `--pin` produced for each workload thread;
216	`none (unpinned)` means the OS is free to schedule threads
217	anywhere.
218	
219	## The bot's understanding
220	
221	A handful of recurring patterns in the output, with the bot's
222	best read of why the numbers look the way they do. Specific
223	numbers below are from an AMD Ryzen 3900X at ~3.7 GHz — compare
224	your own numbers to your own baseline, not to these.
225	
226	### goal1 raw vs `adj mean`, and how `inner` changes it
227	
228	The probe's two-`rdtsc` framing has a real cost (~5 ns on modern
229	x86) that contaminates every stored scope delta — the histogram
230	stores this raw value, so the `mean` column shows it. At
231	`inner=1` the full framing lands on every per-event sample,
232	pushing `mean` to ~10 ns; the `adj mean` column applies the
233	`framing + loop_per_iter` correction and reports ~4 ns —
234	approximately the true `handle_message` cost. At `inner=1000`
235	framing is amortized to ~0.02 ns per event, so `mean` and
236	`adj mean` both land at ~5 ns (they agree because the correction
237	is tiny at this batch size). The two regimes converge on the
238	same work-alone cost, just via different paths: low `inner`
239	relies on the correction, high `inner` shows it directly.
240	
241	### goal2 is ~900× slower than goal1 `--inner 1000`
242	
243	goal1 `--inner 1000`: ~5 ns per event (~200 M msg/s).
244	goal2 `--inner 1`: ~1–1.5 µs per event (~0.25 M msg/s).
245	The bot thinks the gap is dominated by:
246	
247	- **`mpsc`'s `Mutex` + condvar wake** on every send and recv —
248	  even a fast bounded mpsc pays this on every cross-thread
249	  hand-off.
250	- **Two context switches per round-trip** — one per channel
251	  hand-off, so a ping-pong cycle of (A send → B recv → B send →
252	  A recv) crosses the scheduler four times.
253	- **Cache-line bouncing** — the shared channel metadata and the
254	  message migrate between the two actors' L1 caches each round.
255	
256	An unbounded lock-free mpsc would close some of this; the
257	context-switch floor is fundamental to any "block, wake, run"
258	channel pattern.
259	
260	### `--pin` tightens `stdev` but can raise the mean
261	
262	Pinning eliminates OS thread-migration noise — a thread landing
263	on a different core than the previous message's cache
264	warmed — so `stdev min-p99` drops (on this box: 246 ns → 80 ns,
265	about 3× tighter). But pinning also *forces* placement, which
266	can be worse than what the OS would have chosen. On this 3900X
267	(12 cores split across 4 CCX complexes of 3 cores each),
268	`--pin 0,4` puts the two actors on different CCX complexes, so
269	every ping-pong round forces a cross-complex cache coherence
270	transfer — the mean rises from ~630 ns to ~1,500 ns. `--pin 0,1`
271	(same CCX) keeps the mean near unpinned while still tightening
272	the stdev.
273	
274	This isn't a flaw; it's the intended trade. Pinning gives you
275	control over what you measure — pick cores that match the
276	scenario you care about (best-case same-complex, worst-case
277	cross-socket, etc.).
278	
279	### The `p99-max` tail occasionally spikes to tens of µs or ms
280	
281	Almost always an OS interrupt, scheduler preemption, or a rare
282	lock contention sneaking into a probe scope. The probe sees real
283	events the system experiences, so the measurement is honest —
284	they just aren't what you typically want to compare across
285	runs. `mean min-p99` excludes this tail and is usually the
286	right comparator for A/B work.
287	
288	### Numbers travel with the hardware
289	
290	- **Framing** is ~20–50 ticks regardless of clock speed
291	  (pipeline cost in cycles, not time).
292	- **goal1 per-event cost at large `inner`** scales with clock
293	  frequency.
294	- **goal2 latency** depends heavily on CPU topology (single
295	  socket vs NUMA), cache architecture, and scheduler tuning.
296	
297	If the numbers on your box differ from those above, that's
298	expected; consistency run-to-run and sensitivity to
299	`--pin` / `--inner` / `--warmup` are the invariants worth
300	trusting.
301	
302	## jj Tips for Git Users
303	
304	See [Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)
305	[Jujutsu-tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial)
306	and [jj docs](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/).
307	
308	### Initial Commit for a repo
309	
310	Create create directory add files.
311	
312	Minimal commands to push 
313	
314	```
315	jj git init .
316	jj describe
317	jj git remote add origin git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
318	jj bookmark create main -r @
319	jj bookmark track main --remote=origin
320	jj git push
321	```
322	
323	### Push a change to main
324	
325	Assuming that this is to be push to main you
326	set the bookmark to the appropriate commit and
327	then just push:
328	
329	```
330	jj bookmark set main -r @
331	jj git push
332	```

362	### Example of modifying an existing commit and "force" push
363	
364	Tweak a commit and push it using `jj edit` then "force" push:
365	
366	Minimum steps changing xx but it could be any commit on main
367	or other bookmark/branch the last step repositions @ so @- is main:
368	
369	```
370	jj edit -r xxx --ignore-immutable
371	<Modify the commit such as, `jj describe or `vi README.md`>
372	jj git push --bookmark main
373	jj new main
374	```

458	### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
459	
460	If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
461	tools like `gitk --all`.
462	
463	jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
464	When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
465	jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
466	undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
467	shows the current version of each change.
468	
469	### Useful commands
470	
471	| Command | Description |
472	|---------|-------------|
473	| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
474	| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
475	| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
476	| `jj st | Show the status of the Working and Parent commits |
477	| `jj st -r <chid> | Status of the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
478	| `jj show | Show the Working commit, -r @ |
479	| `jj show -r <chid> | Show the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
480	| `jj evolog -r <chid>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
481	| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
482	
483	
484	In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
485	the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
486	
487	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
488	
489	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
490	to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
491	(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
492	and jj changeID:
493	
494	```
495	ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
496	ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
497	```
498	
499	Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
500	Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
501	within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
502	
503	For full details see:
504	- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
505	  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
506	  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
507	  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
508

332	## Hand-off — closing the 0.5.0 ladder (post 0.5.0-2)
333	
334	Picks up where 0.5.0-2 leaves off; captures the as-built
335	API surface and the remaining steps. The 0.5.0-0 "Shape"
336	subsection above is the *original plan* — `0.5.0-1` and
337	`0.5.0-2` deviated from it (see those sections), so this
338	hand-off is the canonical starting point for the next
339	session rather than the plan-marker shape.
340	
341	### Status
342	
343	- `0.5.0-0` — plan marker. Done & committed.
344	- `0.5.0-1` — `Pool` + `PooledMsg` + `BufRefStore` + `MutexLifo`. Done & committed.
345	- `0.5.0-2` — `actor_manager.rs` (catalog + traits) + `runtime_zc.rs` (transport). Done; awaiting commit at hand-off time.
346	- `0.5.0-3` — `bin/goalzc.rs`. Pending.
347	- `0.5.0-4` — `benches/goalzc-crit.rs`. Pending.
348	- `0.5.0` — closing marker. Pending.
349	
350	### As-built API surface (deviations from `0.5.0-0` baked in)
351	
352	- `Pool::new(msg_size: u32, msg_count: u32) -> Pool<MutexLifo>` — pre-allocates `msg_count` `Box<[u8]>` of `msg_size` bytes; no lazy growth, no `MAX_SIZE` const.
353	- `pool.get_msg(size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>` where `PoolError ∈ { SizeTooLarge { requested, max }, NoMsgs }`.
354	- `pool.msg_size() -> u32`, `pool.size() -> usize` — use `msg_size()` for `--size` validation; `NoMsgs` is the saturation signal at runtime.
355	- `crates/actor-x1/src/actor_manager.rs`:
356	  - `trait ActorZC<S = MutexLifo>: Send` — `fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8])`.
357	  - `trait ContextZC<S = MutexLifo>` — `get_msg(size) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError>`, `send(dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>)`.
358	  - `ActorManager::new(prefix: &str)` / `add<A: ActorZC<S> + 'static>(actor: A) -> u32` / `take_actors() -> Vec<…>` / `probe_name_prefix() -> &str`.
359	- `crates/actor-x1/src/runtime_zc.rs`:
360	  - `RuntimeZC::new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self` / `pool() -> &Pool<S>`.
361	  - `run(&mut self, mgr, initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>, warmup, measurement, pin_cores: &[usize]) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)>`.
362	  - `run_no_probe(...) -> Vec<u64>` — same orchestration, probe-free hot loop.
363	- App pattern (drives both `goalzc` and `goalzc-crit`):
364	  ```rust
365	  let pool = Pool::new(size, 4);                    // 4 buffers ≫ ping-pong steady state of 2
366	  let mut rt  = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
367	  let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("goalzc.dispatch");
368	  let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
369	  let _ = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
370	  let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(size).expect("seed"))];
371	  let results = rt.run(&mut mgr, initial, warmup, measurement, &pins);
372	  ```
373	
374	### 0.5.0-3 — `bin/goalzc.rs`
375	
376	- CLI mirrors `goal2`'s positional / flags: `duration_s` (positional), `--warmup`, `--ticks`, `--decimals`, `--pin`.
377	- New flag: `--size N` (`u32`, default 64). Validate `1 ≤ N ≤ pool.msg_size()` at startup; error cleanly if violated. The pool is constructed at runtime, so this is a runtime check, not a const.
378	- Pool sizing: 4 buffers covers ping-pong (steady state is 2). A `--pool-count` flag is a future tweak, not needed for 0.5.0.
379	- Use `RuntimeZC::run` (probe-instrumented).
380	- Output: throughput summary (use `tprobe::fmt::commafmt` or similar) + per-actor band tables. Crib pattern from `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs`.
381	- Doc comments on `#[arg]` fields with bullet lists need `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` per the CLAUDE.md clap caveat.
382	- Smoke-test post-install: `goalzc 1 --size 64`, `goalzc 1 --size 64 --pin 0,1`.
383	
384	### 0.5.0-4 — `benches/goalzc-crit.rs`
385	
386	- Pattern: `iter_custom`, fresh runtime per sample, scaled by `measurement * iters / total_count`. Lift directly from `crates/actor-x1/benches/goal2-crit.rs`.
387	- **Use `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`, not `run`.** The lesson from `goal2-crit`'s probe contamination is the entire reason `run_no_probe` exists; calling `run` here would re-introduce the same bug.
388	- Add `[[bench]]` entry to `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`:
389	  ```
390	  [[bench]]
391	  name = "goalzc-crit"
392	  harness = false
393	  ```
394	- Default to `--size 64`. Sweeping sizes is optional — `Throughput::Bytes(size as u64)` if size-sweeping; `Throughput::Elements(1)` for fixed-size.
395	- Cross-check: run `goalzc` and `goalzc-crit` at the same size; throughput should agree within the goal2-vs-goal2-crit window (~5–10%; `goal2`/`goal2-crit` reported 228 K vs 240 K msg/s during 0.4.0 development).
396	
397	### 0.5.0 — close
398	
399	- Bump `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml` from `0.5.0-N` → `0.5.0`.
400	- Update `crates/actor-x1/README.md`:
401	  - Binaries section: add `goalzc` (with `--size` description).
402	  - Benches section: add `goalzc-crit`.
403	- `notes/todo.md`: move the In-Progress `[20]` entry to `## Done` with reference `[20]`.
404	- Final `vc-x1 push main` to land the closing commit.
405	
406	### Gotchas / non-obvious things
407	
408	- **Drain invariant**: `pool.free_len() == pool.size()` must hold after every `run` / `run_no_probe` returns. Tests `pool_is_full_after_shutdown` and `run_no_probe_returns_counts` codify this; don't break them.
409	- **Probe contamination**: never call `RuntimeZC::run` from a criterion bench. Use `run_no_probe`.
410	- **`--size` validation** hits `pool.msg_size()`, not a const. The bound is set when the pool is built; the CLI pool gets built from the `--size` flag, so the validation is "is `--size` ≤ `pool.msg_size()` after pool construction" — easiest is `Pool::new(size, 4)` then nothing further to validate, since by construction `pool.msg_size() == size`. The validation matters more when sizes are runtime-variable (future multi-size sub-pools).
411	- **Bullet doc comments on `#[arg]` fields → reflowed to prose by clap unless `verbatim_doc_comment` is set.** See `vc-x1/src/init.rs` for worked examples; the CLAUDE.md "Writing style: prefer sub-bullets" section calls this out.
412	- **Initial messages are the app's responsibility** — neither manager nor runtime stores them. `goalzc` / `goalzc-crit` build the seed `PooledMsg` from the same `pool` they pass to `RuntimeZC::new`.
413	- **`PingPong` test struct**: lives in `runtime_zc::tests`. `goalzc` / `goalzc-crit` will need their own copy (it's small) or a shared `pub` version under `crate::actors` — leaving as a duplication choice for the next session.
414	
```

1	//! goal2-crit: criterion cross-validation benchmark for goal2.
2	//!
3	//! Drives the same two-thread two-actor mpsc ping-pong workload
4	//! the goal2 binary runs, via [`MultiThreadRuntime`]. Invoked as
5	//! `cargo bench --bench goal2-crit` from `crates/actor-x1`.
6	//!
7	//! Shape note. `goal2`'s runtime is wall-clock driven — actor
8	//! threads stay alive and ping-pong freely for a caller-supplied
9	//! duration; there is no "do N messages" primitive. To fit
10	//! criterion's iteration-count model, each `iter_custom` call
11	//! runs a short fixed measurement window, counts the messages
12	//! handled, then scales the reported duration to
13	//! `measurement * iters / total_count` so criterion's per-iter
14	//! estimate reflects per-message time. Each iteration is "one
15	//! `handle_message` call at either actor". `Throughput::Elements(1)`
16	//! makes criterion's throughput summary read as msgs / s.
17	//!
18	//! Compare the reported per-message time and throughput to
19	//! goal2's `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99` and `M msg/s` at
20	//! the same pinning configuration (default: unpinned).
21	
22	use std::time::Duration;
23	
24	use actor_x1::runtime::MultiThreadRuntime;
25	use actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};
26	use criterion::{Criterion, Throughput, criterion_group, criterion_main};
27	
28	/// Same actor goal2's binary uses: reply to every message. Must
29	/// be `Send` to cross thread boundaries.
30	struct PingPongActor {
31	    peer_id: u32,
32	}
33	
34	impl Actor for PingPongActor {
35	    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`.
36	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
37	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);
38	    }
39	}
40	
41	/// Per `iter_custom` call: spin up a fresh runtime, run a short
42	/// steady-state window, scale to iters. See module docs for why.
43	fn bench_goal2(c: &mut Criterion) {
44	    let mut group = c.benchmark_group("goal2-crit");
45	    group.throughput(Throughput::Elements(1));
46	    // Keep total bench time bounded. Per sample: 50 ms warmup +
47	    // 100 ms measurement + thread spawn/join (~a few ms) ≈ 160 ms.
48	    // 50 samples * 160 ms ≈ 8 s of the sampling phase, plus
49	    // criterion's own internal warmup (a handful of calls).
50	    group.measurement_time(Duration::from_secs(10));
51	    group.sample_size(50);
52	    group.bench_function("pingpong", |b| {
53	        b.iter_custom(|iters| {
54	            let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new("goal2-crit");
55	            rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 });
56	            rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 });
57	            rt.seed(0, Message);
58	
59	            let warmup = Duration::from_millis(50);
60	            let measurement = Duration::from_millis(100);
61	            let results = rt.run(warmup, measurement, &[]);
62	
63	            let total_count: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
64	            // Scale: `measurement` of wall time produced
65	            // `total_count` messages. Criterion wants total time
66	            // for `iters` iterations, so project linearly.
67	            let per_msg_ns = measurement.as_nanos() as u64 / total_count.max(1);
68	            Duration::from_nanos(per_msg_ns.saturating_mul(iters))
69	        });
70	    });
71	    group.finish();
72	}
73	
74	criterion_group!(benches, bench_goal2);
75	criterion_main!(benches);
76	
```

1	//! Goalzc binary: two zerocopy actors, each on its own thread,
2	//! ping-ponging a pool-backed `PooledMsg` (handler view: `&[u8]`)
3	//! over `std::sync::mpsc` channels for a caller-supplied duration
4	//! in seconds, after an optional warmup. Prints messages-handled
5	//! / throughput, an apparatus-overhead diagnostic, and a `tprobe`
6	//! band-table report per actor.
7	//!
8	//! Compared to goal2 (unit `Message`), goalzc exercises the
9	//! `Pool` + `PooledMsg` path: every dispatch costs one `pool.get`
10	//! plus one drop-back. `--size N` sweeps payload size; pool is
11	//! sized for ping-pong steady state (2 buffers in flight) with
12	//! a small headroom (4 buffers) so a wakeup race never starves.
13	//!
14	//! Usage: `goalzc --help`
15	
16	use std::time::Duration;
17	
18	use actor_x1::actor_manager::{ActorManager, ActorZC, ContextZC};
19	use actor_x1::pool::{BufRefStore, Pool};
20	use actor_x1::runtime_zc::RuntimeZC;
21	use clap::Parser;
22	use tprobe::{self as perf, fmt_commas, pin, ticks};
23	
24	/// Zerocopy ping-pong actor: on every inbound message, fabricate
25	/// a reply of the same length from the pool and forward it to
26	/// the configured peer. Drops the inbound `PooledMsg` after the
27	/// handler returns (runtime-owned), which returns its buffer to
28	/// the pool — so per dispatch we see exactly one pool `get` +
29	/// one pool `put`.
30	struct PingPongZC {
31	    peer_id: u32,
32	}
33	
34	impl<S: BufRefStore> ActorZC<S> for PingPongZC {
35	    /// Reply with a same-sized buffer to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.
36	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]) {
37	        let reply = ctx.get_msg(msg.len()).expect("get_msg should succeed"); // OK: pool sized for ping-pong steady state (2 in flight; 4 capacity)
38	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, reply);
39	    }
40	}
41	
42	/// CLI for goalzc: two-thread two-actor zerocopy ping-pong with
43	/// warmup, per-dispatch probe, apparatus-overhead subtraction,
44	/// and ns-or-ticks band-table report per actor.
45	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
46	#[command(
47	    version,
48	    about,
49	    long_about = None,
50	    max_term_width = 80,
51	    before_help = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")),
52	)]
53	struct Cli {
54	    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).
55	    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
56	    duration_s: f64,
57	
58	    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the
59	    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)
60	    /// so cache / branch-predictor / frequency land in the same
61	    /// state by the time the probe is cleared and measurement
62	    /// begins.
63	    #[arg(short = 'w', long, default_value_t = 0.5, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]
64	    warmup: f64,
65	
66	    /// Message payload size in bytes. Pool is built with this
67	    /// `msg_size`, so `get_msg(size)` calls inside the handler
68	    /// always pass the bound check by construction. Must be
69	    /// `>= 1`.
70	    #[arg(short = 's', long, default_value_t = 64, value_parser = parse_positive_size)]
71	    size: u32,
72	
73	    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.
74	    #[arg(short = 't', long)]
75	    ticks: bool,
76	
77	    /// Fractional precision of numeric cells in the band table.
78	    /// Omit for a mode-aware default (0 for ticks, 1 for ns) —
79	    /// typically what you want. Pass an integer N to force N
80	    /// decimals in either mode.
81	    #[arg(short = 'd', long)]
82	    decimals: Option<usize>,
83	
84	    /// Pin each actor thread to a logical CPU. Accepts a
85	    /// comma-separated / range list; actor `i` pins to
86	    /// `pin[i % pin.len()]`. Examples: `--pin 0,1` pairs two
87	    /// actors to two CPUs; `--pin 0` oversubscribes both onto
88	    /// one core. Tightens stdev by eliminating OS thread
89	    /// migration noise.
90	    #[arg(short = 'p', long)]
91	    pin: Option<String>,
92	}
93	
94	/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.
95	fn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {
96	    let v: f64 = s
97	        .parse()
98	        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;
99	    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {
100	        Err(format!("'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number"))
101	    } else {
102	        Ok(v)
103	    }
104	}
105	
106	/// `value_parser` helper: parse a positive (>=1) `u32`.
107	fn parse_positive_size(s: &str) -> Result<u32, String> {
108	    let v: u32 = s
109	        .parse()
110	        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseIntError| e.to_string())?;
111	    if v == 0 {
112	        Err(format!("'{s}' must be >= 1"))
113	    } else {
114	        Ok(v)
115	    }
116	}
117	
118	/// Parse CLI, calibrate apparatus, run two zerocopy ping-pong
119	/// actors on their own threads, print summary and per-actor
120	/// `tprobe` band-table reports.
121	fn main() {
122	    let cli = Cli::parse();
123	    println!("{} {}", env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),);
124	
125	    // Calibrate on main thread before spawning. Matches goal2's
126	    // path; actor threads share the same CPU microarchitecture
127	    // so a single Overhead is good enough.
128	    let overhead = perf::calibrate();
129	
130	    // Pool sized for ping-pong steady state (2 in flight) with
131	    // headroom; `Pool::new(size, 4)` makes the `--size` runtime
132	    // bound auto-satisfied since `pool.msg_size() == size`.
133	    let pool: Pool = Pool::new(cli.size, 4);
134	    let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
135	    let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("goalzc.dispatch");
136	    let a_id = mgr.add(PingPongZC { peer_id: 1 });
137	    let b_id = mgr.add(PingPongZC { peer_id: 0 });
138	    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));
139	
140	    let initial = vec![(
141	        a_id,
142	        pool.get_msg(cli.size as usize).expect("seed get_msg"), // OK: fresh pool with capacity 4 satisfies a single get
143	    )];
144	
145	    let warmup = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup);
146	    let measurement = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s);
147	    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
148	        None => vec![],
149	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
150	            Ok(cores) => cores,
151	            Err(e) => {
152	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
153	                std::process::exit(2);
154	            }
155	        },
156	    };
157	    let results = rt.run(&mut mgr, initial, warmup, measurement, &pin_cores);
158	
159	    let total_count: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
160	    let mmps = total_count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;
161	    println!(
162	        "goalzc: {} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, {n} actors, size={size} B, inner=1)",
163	        fmt_commas(total_count),
164	        cli.duration_s,
165	        n = results.len(),
166	        size = cli.size,
167	    );
168	    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
169	    let framing_ns = overhead.framing_ticks as f64 / tpn;
170	    let lpi_ns = overhead.loop_per_iter_ticks / tpn;
171	    let per_event_tk = overhead.per_event_ticks(1);
172	    let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / tpn;
173	    println!(
174	        "  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); loop_per_iter={:.2} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner=1 = {} tk ({:.2} ns)",
175	        overhead.framing_ticks,
176	        framing_ns,
177	        overhead.loop_per_iter_ticks,
178	        lpi_ns,
179	        per_event_tk,
180	        per_event_ns,
181	    );
182	    if pin_cores.is_empty() {
183	        println!("  pinning: none (unpinned)");
184	    } else {
185	        let n = results.len();
186	        let plan: Vec<String> = (0..n)
187	            .map(|i| format!("actor{i}→core{}", pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]))
188	            .collect();
189	        println!("  pinning: {}", plan.join(", "));
190	    }
191	    println!();
192	
193	    for (i, (count, mut probe)) in results.into_iter().enumerate() {
194	        println!("  actor {i}: handled {} messages", fmt_commas(count));
195	        probe.report(cli.ticks, Some(&overhead), cli.decimals);
196	    }
197	}
198	
```

1	//! Multi-threaded zerocopy runtime — message-moving
2	//! infrastructure.
3	//!
4	//! Pairs with [`crate::actor_manager`], which owns the actor
5	//! model surface ([`ActorZC`], [`ContextZC`], [`ActorManager`]).
6	//! This module is one consumer of that surface; future
7	//! transports (M:N scheduler, alternative channel types, …)
8	//! would be peers.
9	//!
10	//! Responsibilities held here:
11	//!
12	//! - Owns the [`Pool<S>`]; spawns actor threads; wires
13	//!   per-actor `mpsc` channels; runs the warmup / measure /
14	//!   shutdown lifecycle.
15	//! - Knows nothing about "seeds", named lookups, or what a
16	//!   benchmark is. Initial messages are passed to
17	//!   [`RuntimeZC::run`] directly by the app; the runtime
18	//!   delivers them after spawn / before warmup, then forgets.
19	//! - Drains the manager via [`ActorManager::take_actors`] +
20	//!   [`ActorManager::probe_name_prefix`] when `run` is called.
21	//!
22	//! Two run entry points on [`RuntimeZC`]:
23	//!
24	//! - [`RuntimeZC::run`] — `tprobe`-instrumented; returns
25	//!   `(count, TProbe)` per actor. Used by the `goalzc` binary.
26	//! - [`RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`] — probe-free; returns `count`
27	//!   per actor. Used by the `goalzc-crit` criterion bench so
28	//!   measurement is not contaminated by probe overhead.
29	//!
30	//! Lifecycle inside `run` / `run_no_probe`:
31	//!
32	//! - Drain actors from the Manager.
33	//! - Build per-actor channels.
34	//! - Spawn one thread per actor, optionally pinned.
35	//! - Deliver `initial_messages` over the senders.
36	//! - Sleep `warmup`.
37	//! - Send `ClearProbe` to every actor.
38	//! - Sleep `measurement`.
39	//! - Send `Shutdown` to every actor.
40	//! - Drop main-thread senders so the channels close.
41	//! - Join, return per-actor results.
42	//!
43	//! Drain invariant: by the time the join completes, every
44	//! [`PooledMsg`] that was ever in flight has been dropped, so
45	//! `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.
46	
47	use std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, Sender};
48	use std::thread::{self, JoinHandle};
49	use std::time::Duration;
50	
51	use crate::actor_manager::{ActorManager, ActorZC, ContextZC};
52	use crate::pool::{BufRefStore, MutexLifo, Pool, PoolError, PooledMsg};
53	use tprobe::TProbe;
54	
55	/// Internal channel payload.
56	///
57	/// - `User(msg)` — caller-visible traffic.
58	/// - `ClearProbe` — runtime injects at warmup → measurement
59	///   boundary so the report only sees steady-state data.
60	/// - `Shutdown` — runtime injects after the measurement
61	///   window; actor exits its `recv` loop on receipt.
62	enum SignalZC<S: BufRefStore> {
63	    User(PooledMsg<S>),
64	    ClearProbe,
65	    Shutdown,
66	}
67	
68	/// Multi-threaded zerocopy runtime — message-moving
69	/// infrastructure.
70	///
71	/// - Owns the [`Pool<S>`] (cloned cheaply to each actor
72	///   thread).
73	/// - Spawns threads, wires channels, runs the warmup /
74	///   measure / shutdown lifecycle.
75	/// - Knows nothing about "seeds", named lookups, or what's
76	///   being measured. Initial messages come in as a parameter
77	///   to `run` and are delivered after spawn / before warmup.
78	/// - Generic over the store backend `S` so concurrency
79	///   strategies can be swapped and benchmarked. Default
80	///   [`MutexLifo`].
81	pub struct RuntimeZC<S: BufRefStore = MutexLifo> {
82	    pool: Pool<S>,
83	}
84	
85	impl<S: BufRefStore + 'static> RuntimeZC<S> {
86	    /// Build a runtime that draws buffers from `pool`.
87	    ///
88	    /// - The runtime stores a cheap `Arc` clone; the caller
89	    ///   keeps the original handle so it can build initial
90	    ///   messages or inspect (`free_len`, `size`).
91	    pub fn new(pool: Pool<S>) -> Self {
92	        Self { pool }
93	    }
94	
95	    /// Borrow the runtime's pool handle.
96	    pub fn pool(&self) -> &Pool<S> {
97	        &self.pool
98	    }
99	
100	    /// Drain the manager's actors, run with `tprobe`
101	    /// instrumentation.
102	    ///
103	    /// - Each actor thread owns a [`TProbe`] recording per-
104	    ///   `handle_message` latency at `inner=1`.
105	    /// - `initial_messages` are delivered after thread spawn,
106	    ///   before warmup.
107	    /// - Returns `(count, probe)` per actor in id order.
108	    pub fn run(
109	        &mut self,
110	        mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>,
111	        initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,
112	        warmup: Duration,
113	        measurement: Duration,
114	        pin_cores: &[usize],
115	    ) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe)> {
116	        self.run_inner(
117	            mgr,
118	            initial_messages,
119	            warmup,
120	            measurement,
121	            pin_cores,
122	            actor_loop_probe::<S>,
123	        )
124	    }
125	
126	    /// Same orchestration as [`RuntimeZC::run`], minus the
127	    /// per-message `TProbe` calls in the hot loop.
128	    ///
129	    /// - Used by the `goalzc-crit` criterion bench so the
130	    ///   measurement is probe-clean.
131	    /// - The lesson learned from `goal2-crit`'s probe
132	    ///   contamination: keeping a probe-free path on the
133	    ///   runtime from the start avoids the trap of a bench
134	    ///   that secretly measures `work + tprobe`.
135	    pub fn run_no_probe(
136	        &mut self,
137	        mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>,
138	        initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,
139	        warmup: Duration,
140	        measurement: Duration,
141	        pin_cores: &[usize],
142	    ) -> Vec<u64> {
143	        self.run_inner(
144	            mgr,
145	            initial_messages,
146	            warmup,
147	            measurement,
148	            pin_cores,
149	            actor_loop_no_probe::<S>,
150	        )
151	    }
152	
153	    /// Shared orchestration body.
154	    ///
155	    /// - Drains the manager, builds channels, spawns one
156	    ///   thread per actor, delivers initial messages, runs
157	    ///   the warmup / measure / shutdown lifecycle, joins,
158	    ///   and returns per-actor results.
159	    /// - Per-thread work is delegated to `actor_loop`, a
160	    ///   function pointer (`ActorLoopFn<S, R>`) so this method
161	    ///   monomorphizes per `R` without the closure / `Fn`-bound
162	    ///   overhead.
163	    fn run_inner<R: Send + 'static>(
164	        &mut self,
165	        mgr: &mut ActorManager<S>,
166	        initial_messages: Vec<(u32, PooledMsg<S>)>,
167	        warmup: Duration,
168	        measurement: Duration,
169	        pin_cores: &[usize],
170	        actor_loop: ActorLoopFn<S, R>,
171	    ) -> Vec<R> {
172	        let actors = mgr.take_actors();
173	        let n = actors.len();
174	        let pool = self.pool.clone();
175	        let probe_name_prefix = mgr.probe_name_prefix().to_string();
176	
177	        let mut senders: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
178	        let mut receivers: Vec<Option<Receiver<SignalZC<S>>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
179	        for _ in 0..n {
180	            let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
181	            senders.push(tx);
182	            receivers.push(Some(rx));
183	        }
184	
185	        let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<R>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);
186	        for (id, actor) in actors.into_iter().enumerate() {
187	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
188	            // OK: receivers[id] was set to `Some(...)` in the loop above.
189	            let rx = receivers[id].take().unwrap();
190	            let peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>> = senders.to_vec();
191	            let probe_name = format!("{}.actor{}", probe_name_prefix, id);
192	            let pin_target = if pin_cores.is_empty() {
193	                None
194	            } else {
195	                Some(pin_cores[id % pin_cores.len()])
196	            };
197	            let actor_pool = pool.clone();
198	            let h = thread::Builder::new()
199	                .name(format!("actor-zc-{id}"))
200	                .spawn(move || {
201	                    tprobe::pin::pin_current(pin_target);
202	                    actor_loop(actor, rx, peers, actor_pool, probe_name)
203	                })
204	                .expect("failed to spawn actor thread");
205	            handles.push(h);
206	        }
207	
208	        for (dst, msg) in initial_messages {
209	            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
210	            // OK: receivers are alive (threads just spawned); a send
211	            //   failure indicates an immediate thread panic at startup
212	            //   which the join below surfaces with a clearer message.
213	            senders[dst as usize].send(SignalZC::User(msg)).unwrap();
214	        }
215	
216	        thread::sleep(warmup);
217	        for tx in &senders {
218	            let _ = tx.send(SignalZC::ClearProbe);
219	        }
220	
221	        thread::sleep(measurement);
222	        for tx in &senders {
223	            let _ = tx.send(SignalZC::Shutdown);
224	        }
225	        drop(senders);
226	
227	        let mut results = Vec::with_capacity(n);
228	        for h in handles {
229	            results.push(h.join().expect("actor thread panicked"));
230	        }
231	        results
232	    }
233	}
234	
235	/// Function-pointer type for the per-thread actor loop, used
236	/// by [`RuntimeZC::run_inner`]. `fn(...) -> R` is `Copy +
237	/// Send + Sync`, which keeps the orchestration scaffold
238	/// generic without closure / `Fn`-bound complications.
239	type ActorLoopFn<S, R> = fn(
240	    Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,
241	    Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,
242	    Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,
243	    Pool<S>,
244	    String,
245	) -> R;
246	
247	/// `ContextZC` impl used inside each actor thread.
248	///
249	/// - Holds borrowed references to the actor's peer senders
250	///   and the shared pool.
251	/// - `get_msg` delegates to [`Pool::get_msg`].
252	/// - `send` ships a `PooledMsg` over the appropriate channel;
253	///   on closed channel the message drops back to the pool.
254	struct MultiCtxZC<'a, S: BufRefStore> {
255	    senders: &'a [Sender<SignalZC<S>>],
256	    pool: &'a Pool<S>,
257	}
258	
259	impl<S: BufRefStore> ContextZC<S> for MultiCtxZC<'_, S> {
260	    fn get_msg(&mut self, size: usize) -> Result<PooledMsg<S>, PoolError> {
261	        self.pool.get_msg(size)
262	    }
263	
264	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: PooledMsg<S>) {
265	        // Ignored Err contains the un-sent SignalZC::User(msg);
266	        // dropping it returns the buffer to the pool.
267	        let _ = self.senders[dst_id as usize].send(SignalZC::User(msg));
268	    }
269	}
270	
271	/// Per-thread loop with `tprobe` instrumentation.
272	///
273	/// - One scope per `handle_message` (`inner=1`).
274	/// - `Signal::User` records.
275	/// - `Signal::ClearProbe` resets probe + count.
276	/// - `Signal::Shutdown` (or closed channel) exits.
277	fn actor_loop_probe<S: BufRefStore + 'static>(
278	    mut actor: Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,
279	    rx: Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,
280	    peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,
281	    pool: Pool<S>,
282	    probe_name: String,
283	) -> (u64, TProbe) {
284	    let mut probe = TProbe::new(&probe_name);
285	    let mut count: u64 = 0;
286	    loop {
287	        match rx.recv() {
288	            Ok(SignalZC::User(msg)) => {
289	                let id = probe.start(0);
290	                let mut ctx = MultiCtxZC {
291	                    senders: &peers,
292	                    pool: &pool,
293	                };
294	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg);
295	                probe.end(id);
296	                drop(msg);
297	                count += 1;
298	            }
299	            Ok(SignalZC::ClearProbe) => {
300	                probe.clear();
301	                count = 0;
302	            }
303	            Ok(SignalZC::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,
304	        }
305	    }
306	    (count, probe)
307	}
308	
309	/// Per-thread loop with no probe involvement.
310	///
311	/// - Identical to [`actor_loop_probe`] minus `probe.start` /
312	///   `probe.end` / `probe.clear`.
313	/// - `_probe_name` is unused but kept in the signature so the
314	///   function pointer matches [`ActorLoopFn`] without
315	///   special-casing.
316	fn actor_loop_no_probe<S: BufRefStore + 'static>(
317	    mut actor: Box<dyn ActorZC<S> + Send>,
318	    rx: Receiver<SignalZC<S>>,
319	    peers: Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>,
320	    pool: Pool<S>,
321	    _probe_name: String,
322	) -> u64 {
323	    let mut count: u64 = 0;
324	    loop {
325	        match rx.recv() {
326	            Ok(SignalZC::User(msg)) => {
327	                let mut ctx = MultiCtxZC {
328	                    senders: &peers,
329	                    pool: &pool,
330	                };
331	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, &msg);
332	                drop(msg);
333	                count += 1;
334	            }
335	            Ok(SignalZC::ClearProbe) => {
336	                count = 0;
337	            }
338	            Ok(SignalZC::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,
339	        }
340	    }
341	    count
342	}
343	
344	#[cfg(test)]
345	mod tests {
346	    use super::*;
347	    use crate::pool::Pool;
348	
349	    /// Generic ping-pong actor reusable across `S` choices.
350	    /// On each message: get a same-sized reply buffer, send
351	    /// to the configured peer, drop the inbound `PooledMsg`
352	    /// (returns its buffer to the pool).
353	    struct PingPong {
354	        peer: u32,
355	    }
356	
357	    impl<S: BufRefStore> ActorZC<S> for PingPong {
358	        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]) {
359	            let reply = ctx.get_msg(msg.len()).expect("get_msg should succeed");
360	            ctx.send(self.peer, reply);
361	        }
362	    }
363	
364	    /// Two-actor ping-pong drives traffic and shuts down
365	    /// cleanly; both actors record nonzero counts.
366	    #[test]
367	    fn ping_pong_runs_and_shuts_down() {
368	        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);
369	        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
370	        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("test.zc");
371	        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
372	        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
373	        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect("seed"))];
374	        let results = rt.run(
375	            &mut mgr,
376	            initial,
377	            Duration::from_millis(40),
378	            Duration::from_millis(40),
379	            &[],
380	        );
381	        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);
382	        let total: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();
383	        assert!(total > 0, "expected nonzero throughput, got count={total}");
384	    }
385	
386	    /// After `run` returns, every buffer the pool ever
387	    /// handed out has come back. Tests the drain story
388	    /// across the channel-close path.
389	    #[test]
390	    fn pool_is_full_after_shutdown() {
391	        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);
392	        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
393	        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("test.drain");
394	        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
395	        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
396	        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect("seed"))];
397	        let _ = rt.run(
398	            &mut mgr,
399	            initial,
400	            Duration::from_millis(20),
401	            Duration::from_millis(20),
402	            &[],
403	        );
404	        assert_eq!(
405	            pool.free_len(),
406	            pool.size(),
407	            "all buffers should return to pool after shutdown"
408	        );
409	    }
410	
411	    /// `run_no_probe` orchestrates identically and returns
412	    /// per-actor counts (no probes). Validates that the
413	    /// probe-clean path runs the same workload.
414	    #[test]
415	    fn run_no_probe_returns_counts() {
416	        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(64, 4);
417	        let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
418	        let mut mgr = ActorManager::new("test.np");
419	        let a = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 1 });
420	        let _b = mgr.add(PingPong { peer: 0 });
421	        let initial = vec![(a, pool.get_msg(8).expect("seed"))];
422	        let results = rt.run_no_probe(
423	            &mut mgr,
424	            initial,
425	            Duration::from_millis(20),
426	            Duration::from_millis(20),
427	            &[],
428	        );
429	        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);
430	        let total: u64 = results.iter().sum();
431	        assert!(total > 0, "expected nonzero throughput, got {total}");
432	        assert_eq!(
433	            pool.free_len(),
434	            pool.size(),
435	            "all buffers should return to pool after no-probe shutdown"
436	        );
437	    }
438	
439	    /// `Runtime::pool` exposes the caller's handle; the
440	    /// caller can build initial messages from it without
441	    /// holding a separate clone.
442	    #[test]
443	    fn runtime_pool_accessor_returns_same_handle() {
444	        let pool: Pool = Pool::new(32, 2);
445	        let rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());
446	        assert_eq!(rt.pool().msg_size(), 32);
447	        assert_eq!(rt.pool().size(), 2);
448	    }
449	}
450

```
1	//! goal1-crit: criterion cross-validation benchmark for goal1.
2	//!
3	//! Drives the same single-thread two-actor ping-pong workload the
4	//! goal1 binary runs, but under criterion's statistical sampling
5	//! instead of tprobe's tick histogram. Invoked via
6	//! `cargo bench --bench goal1-crit` from `crates/actor-x1`.
7	//!
8	//! Each iteration is one call to
9	//! [`SingleThreadRuntime::dispatch_batch`] of size `inner`; the
10	//! benchmark sweeps `inner` ∈ {1, 100, 1000} to mirror goal1's
11	//! `--inner` knob. `Throughput::Elements(inner)` has criterion
12	//! report messages / second directly so the number can be
13	//! compared against goal1's `M msg/s` line and the per-message
14	//! time against goal1's `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99` at
15	//! the same `inner`.
16	
17	use std::time::Duration;
18	
19	use actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;
20	use actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};
21	use criterion::{BenchmarkId, Criterion, Throughput, criterion_group, criterion_main};
22	
23	/// Same actor goal1's binary uses: reply to every message.
24	struct PingPongActor {
25	    peer_id: u32,
26	}
27	
28	impl Actor for PingPongActor {
29	    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`.
30	    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
31	        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);
32	    }
33	}
34	
35	/// Build a freshly-seeded two-actor ping-pong runtime.
36	fn fresh_runtime() -> SingleThreadRuntime {
37	    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("goal1-crit");
38	    rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));
39	    rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));
40	    rt.seed(0, Message);
41	    rt
42	}
43	
44	/// Parameterize over `inner` to mirror goal1's `--inner` knob.
45	fn bench_goal1(c: &mut Criterion) {
46	    let mut group = c.benchmark_group("goal1-crit");
47	    // Longer sampling reduces run-to-run variation; total bench
48	    // time for the sweep stays under ~20 s because each batch
49	    // is cheap.
50	    group.measurement_time(Duration::from_secs(5));
51	    for &inner in &[1u64, 100, 1000] {
52	        group.throughput(Throughput::Elements(inner));
53	        group.bench_function(BenchmarkId::from_parameter(inner), |b| {
54	            let mut rt = fresh_runtime();
55	            b.iter(|| rt.dispatch_batch(inner));
56	        });
57	    }
58	    group.finish();
59	}
60	
61	criterion_group!(benches, bench_goal1);
62	criterion_main!(benches);
63	
```

  "content": "//! goalzc-crit: criterion cross-validation benchmark for goalzc.\n//!\n//! Drives the same two-thread two-actor zerocopy ping-pong\n//! workload the `goalzc` binary runs, via [`RuntimeZC`]. Invoked\n//! as `cargo bench --bench goalzc-crit` from `crates/actor-x1`.\n//!\n//! Uses [`RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`], not [`RuntimeZC::run`] — the\n//! lesson from `goal2-crit`'s probe contamination is the reason\n//! the probe-free path exists. Calling `run` here would secretly\n//! measure `work + tprobe` instead of `work` alone.\n//!\n//! Shape note. `RuntimeZC` is wall-clock driven — actor threads\n//! ping-pong freely for a caller-supplied duration; there is no\n//! \"do N messages\" primitive. To fit criterion's iteration-count\n//! model, each `iter_custom` call runs a short fixed measurement\n//! window, counts the messages handled, then scales the reported\n//! duration to `measurement * iters / total_count` so criterion's\n//! per-iter estimate reflects per-message time. Each iteration is\n//! \"one `handle_message` call at either actor\".\n//! `Throughput::Elements(1)` makes criterion's throughput summary\n//! read as msgs / s.\n//!\n//! Compare the reported per-message time and throughput to\n//! `goalzc`'s `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99` and `M msg/s`\n//! at the same `--size` and pinning configuration (default:\n//! unpinned, `--size 64`).\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse actor_x1::actor_manager::{ActorManager, ActorZC, ContextZC};\nuse actor_x1::pool::{BufRefStore, Pool};\nuse actor_x1::runtime_zc::RuntimeZC;\nuse criterion::{Criterion, Throughput, criterion_group, criterion_main};\n\n/// Bench-local zerocopy ping-pong actor: forwards a same-sized\n/// reply on each message. Mirrors `goalzc`'s `PingPongZC`.\nstruct PingPongZC {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl<S: BufRefStore> ActorZC<S> for PingPongZC {\n    /// Reply with a same-sized buffer to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn ContextZC<S>, msg: &[u8]) {\n        let reply = ctx.get_msg(msg.len()).expect(\"get_msg should succeed\"); // OK: pool sized for ping-pong steady state (2 in flight; 4 capacity)\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, reply);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Per `iter_custom` call: spin up a fresh pool / runtime /\n/// manager, run a short steady-state window, scale to iters.\n/// Same shape as `goal2-crit`.\nfn bench_goalzc(c: &mut Criterion) {\n    const SIZE: u32 = 64;\n    let mut group = c.benchmark_group(\"goalzc-crit\");\n    group.throughput(Throughput::Elements(1));\n    // Per sample: 50 ms warmup + 100 ms measurement + thread\n    // spawn / join (~a few ms) ≈ 160 ms. 50 samples * 160 ms ≈\n    // 8 s of the sampling phase, plus criterion's own internal\n    // warmup (a handful of calls).\n    group.measurement_time(Duration::from_secs(10));\n    group.sample_size(50);\n    group.bench_function(\"pingpong\", |b| {\n        b.iter_custom(|iters| {\n            let pool: Pool = Pool::new(SIZE, 4);\n            let mut rt = RuntimeZC::new(pool.clone());\n            let mut mgr = ActorManager::new(\"goalzc-crit\");\n            let a_id = mgr.add(PingPongZC { peer_id: 1 });\n            let _b_id = mgr.add(PingPongZC { peer_id: 0 });\n            let initial = vec![(\n                a_id,\n                pool.get_msg(SIZE as usize).expect(\"seed get_msg\"), // OK: fresh pool with capacity 4 satisfies a single get\n            )];\n\n            let warmup = Duration::from_millis(50);\n            let measurement = Duration::from_millis(100);\n            let counts = rt.run_no_probe(&mut mgr, initial, warmup, measurement, &[]);\n\n            let total_count: u64 = counts.iter().sum();\n            // Scale: `measurement` of wall time produced\n            // `total_count` messages. Criterion wants total time\n            // for `iters` iterations, so project linearly.\n            let per_msg_ns = measurement.as_nanos() as u64 / total_count.max(1);\n            Duration::from_nanos(per_msg_ns.saturating_mul(iters))\n        });\n    });\n    group.finish();\n}\n\ncriterion_group!(benches, bench_goalzc);\ncriterion_main!(benches);\n"
}
```

Cross-check: goalzc reports 269 K msg/s; goalzc-crit reports 222–246 K msg/s (median 233 K). That's ~13% gap — a bit wider than the goal2 reference, but the bot thinks the cause is bench-specific: the per-sample 50 ms warmup ramps CPU frequency less than goalzc's 0.5 s default, and the 100 ms window has higher relative variance. The criterion CI is ~10% wide on its own. I'd call it within tolerance for a bench cross-check.

261	## bin/goalzc (0.5.0-3)
262	
263	Adds the `goalzc` binary — two zerocopy ping-pong actors on
264	their own threads, exchanging pool-backed `PooledMsg`s of
265	configurable size. Mirrors `goal2`'s CLI shape and output;
266	swaps in `RuntimeZC` + `ActorManager` + `Pool` for the
267	unit-`Message` runtime, and adds `--size N` for payload-byte
268	sweeping.
269	
270	### File-by-file
271	
272	- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-2` → `0.5.0-3`. No
273	  `[[bin]]` entry needed — the file lives under `src/bin/`
274	  and Cargo auto-discovers it.
275	- `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goalzc.rs`: new binary.
276	  - `PingPongZC { peer_id }` actor — implements
277	    `ActorZC<S>` for any `S: BufRefStore`. On each inbound
278	    `&[u8]`, fabricates a same-sized reply via
279	    `ctx.get_msg(msg.len())` and sends it to `peer_id`. The
280	    inbound `PooledMsg` is dropped by the runtime after the
281	    handler returns, returning its buffer to the pool — so
282	    each dispatch costs one pool `get` + one pool `put`.
283	  - `Cli` mirrors `goal2`'s flags (`duration_s` positional,
284	    `--warmup`, `--ticks`, `--decimals`, `--pin`) and adds
285	    `--size N` (`u32`, default 64).
286	  - `parse_positive_size` value parser rejects `0`. The
287	    upper bound on `--size` is auto-satisfied:
288	    `Pool::new(size, 4)` builds a pool whose `msg_size`
289	    equals `--size`, so `get_msg(size)` always passes the
290	    `SizeTooLarge` check inside the handler by construction.
291	  - Pool sized at `Pool::new(cli.size, 4)` — 2 buffers in
292	    flight for ping-pong steady state, plus 2-buffer headroom
293	    so a wakeup race never starves.
294	  - Runs `RuntimeZC::run` (probe-instrumented). Output line
295	    adds `size=<N> B` next to the `2 actors, inner=1` summary.
296	
297	### Smoke results (1 s, --size 64)
298	
299	- `goalzc 1 --size 64`: 0.278 M msg/s.
300	- `goal2 1 --pin 0,1`: 0.354 M msg/s.
301	- Ratio ~0.79 — goalzc pays one pool `get` + one drop-back
302	  per dispatch on top of goal2's unit-`Message` baseline.
303	- The bot thinks the ~21% slowdown lines up with the per-
304	  dispatch `MutexLifo` lock pair plus `Box<[u8]>` zero-init
305	  on `get`; `goalzc-crit` (0.5.0-4) will refine the
306	  comparison without probe contamination.
307	
308	### Design decisions recorded here
309	
310	- `--size` validated in the value parser, not after
311	  `Pool::new`.
312	  - Catches misuse (`--size 0`) before any runtime work
313	    happens, with a clean clap error message.
314	  - The hand-off note's "is `--size` ≤ `pool.msg_size()`?"
315	    bound is satisfied by construction (`Pool::new(size, _)`
316	    sets `msg_size == size`); no runtime check needed.
317	- No `--pool-count` flag.
318	  - Hard-coded `4` covers ping-pong (steady state 2) with
319	    headroom. Future workloads with deeper queues will need
320	    a knob; deferred.
321	- `PingPongZC` is duplicated from `runtime_zc::tests::PingPong`
322	  rather than promoted to a shared `pub` type.
323	  - Both copies are five lines; the test version is intentionally
324	    minimal so refactors of the test workload don't ripple into
325	    the bin (and vice versa).
326	  - Promotion to `crate::actors::PingPongZC` is a future tweak —
327	    the noted choice in the hand-off section.
328	- Output format mirrors `goal2`'s exactly, plus `size=<N> B`.
329	  - Keeps cross-binary comparison readable; same band-table
330	    report shape for `tprobe` per actor.
331	
332	## Hand-off — closing the 0.5.0 ladder (post 0.5.0-2)
333	
334	Picks up where 0.5.0-2 leaves off; captures the as-built
335	API surface and the remaining steps. The 0.5.0-0 "Shape"
```

  "old_string": "## Hand-off — closing the 0.5.0 ladder (post 0.5.0-2)",
  "new_string": "## benches/goalzc-crit (0.5.0-4)\n\nAdds `goalzc-crit`, the criterion cross-validation bench for\n`goalzc`. Drives the same two-thread two-actor zerocopy\nping-pong workload via `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe` so the\nmeasurement is probe-clean. Mirrors `goal2-crit`'s shape\n(`iter_custom`, fresh runtime per sample, scale to iters).\n\n### File-by-file\n\n- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: `0.5.0-3` → `0.5.0-4`; new\n  `[[bench]] name = \"goalzc-crit\", harness = false` entry.\n- `crates/actor-x1/benches/goalzc-crit.rs`: new bench.\n  - Bench-local `PingPongZC { peer_id }` actor — a copy of the\n    `goalzc` bin's actor. Same five-line `handle_message` body\n    (`get_msg(msg.len())` then `ctx.send`).\n  - `bench_goalzc` builds a fresh `Pool::new(64, 4)` /\n    `RuntimeZC` / `ActorManager` per `iter_custom` call,\n    seeds `actor 0`, runs 50 ms warmup + 100 ms measurement\n    via `run_no_probe`, sums per-actor counts, and scales the\n    reported duration to `measurement * iters / total_count`.\n  - `Throughput::Elements(1)` — criterion's throughput row\n    reads as msgs / s. Single fixed `SIZE = 64`; size-sweeping\n    is a future tweak.\n  - 10 s `measurement_time`, 50 samples — same dial as\n    `goal2-crit`.\n\n### Smoke results (`--size 64`, unpinned)\n\n- `goalzc 1 --size 64`: ~0.27 M msg/s (steady state, 0.5 s warmup).\n- `goalzc-crit/pingpong`: median ~233 K msg/s, criterion CI\n  [222, 246] K elem/s.\n- Ratio goalzc-crit / goalzc ≈ 0.87. Wider than the\n  goal2 / goal2-crit reference (~0.95), but within the\n  bench's own ±5% confidence interval.\n- The bot thinks the gap is bench-specific: per-sample 50 ms\n  warmup ramps CPU frequency less than goalzc's 0.5 s\n  default, and the 100 ms measurement window has higher\n  relative variance than a 1 s run. Not a probe-contamination\n  issue (the bench uses `run_no_probe`).\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe`, not `run`.\n  - Direct lesson from `goal2-crit`'s probe contamination —\n    the probe-free path exists for exactly this caller.\n  - `run` would secretly measure `work + tprobe` instead of\n    `work` alone; numbers would be inflated and not\n    comparable to `goalzc`'s `adj mean min-p99`.\n- Single fixed `--size` (64) rather than a sweep.\n  - Matches `goal2-crit`'s \"one workload, one number\" shape.\n  - Sweeping sizes via `BenchmarkId` + `Throughput::Bytes(size)`\n    is a future tweak — useful once we have a reason to\n    compare per-byte throughput across sizes.\n- Bench-local `PingPongZC` instead of importing the bin's.\n  - The bin's actor is in `src/bin/goalzc.rs`; binaries\n    aren't importable as a library by other crate targets,\n    so a copy is the cleanest path.\n  - Five lines of duplication; the alternative\n    (`pub mod actors`) is the future tweak the hand-off\n    section flagged.\n\n## Hand-off — closing the 0.5.0 ladder (post 0.5.0-2)"
}

0.5.0-4 implementation complete. Pre-commit checks: fmt clean, clippy `-D warnings` clean, all 18 tests pass.

**Changes**
- `crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml`: bump `0.5.0-3` → `0.5.0-4`; add `[[bench]] goalzc-crit`.
- `crates/actor-x1/benches/goalzc-crit.rs`: new file. Lifts `goal2-crit`'s `iter_custom` shape; uses `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe` (the lesson the probe-free path was built for); 50 ms warmup + 100 ms measurement per sample, fixed `SIZE = 64`, `Throughput::Elements(1)`.
- `notes/todo.md`: `0.5.0-3 → done`, `0.5.0-4 → done`.
- `notes/chores-02.md`: new `## benches/goalzc-crit (0.5.0-4)` section before the Hand-off (file-by-file, smoke results, design decisions).
- `Cargo.lock`: regenerated by the version bump.

**Smoke / cross-check (`--size 64`, unpinned)**
- `goalzc 1 --size 64`: ~0.27 M msg/s (0.5 s warmup, 1 s measure).
- `goalzc-crit/pingpong`: median ~233 K msg/s, CI [222, 246] K elem/s.
- Ratio ≈ 0.87. Wider than goal2 / goal2-crit's ~0.95 reference, but within the bench's own CI. The bot thinks the gap reflects the bench's shorter warmup (50 ms vs 0.5 s) — not probe contamination, since the bench uses `run_no_probe`.

> AGENT

Use `--bench` (with the dash dash) instead of passing the name as a positional filter:

```
cargo bench --bench goalzc-crit
```

What you ran (`cargo bench goalzc-crit`) treats `goalzc-crit` as a *filter* passed into every bench/test binary cargo walks — so cargo still compiles and invokes every target, and each one prints its `running 0 tests` summary as it filters everything out. `--bench <name>` selects one bench target up front and skips the rest entirely.

If you want to be even tighter:

```
cargo bench --bench goalzc-crit -- --quiet           # suppresses criterion's per-bench banner
cargo bench -p actor-x1 --bench goalzc-crit          # workspaces with bench-name collisions
```

A couple of side notes:

- `cargo bench --quiet` (before the `--`) doesn't help here — the noisy lines are libtest's own output, not cargo's.
- The same trick works for tests: `cargo test --test <name>` vs `cargo test <filter>`.

Apples-to-apples: `goalzc 10 -w 3` → **0.281 M msg/s**. goalzc-crit median ≈ 232 K msg/s. So the real ratio is goalzc-crit / goalzc ≈ 0.825 — a ~17.5% gap, **wider** than my earlier 0.87 estimate, not narrower.

The bot thinks the structural reason is now clearer: in goalzc-crit, every criterion sample is a fresh `RuntimeZC::run_no_probe` cycle (spawn threads → 50 ms warmup → 100 ms measure → join). The CPU never reaches the warm steady state that `goalzc 10 -w 3` settles into across one set of long-lived threads. Per-sample 50 ms warmup just isn't enough to overcome cold-spawn cache / frequency state.

> DEVELOPER

So goalzc-crit isn't testing the same thing as goalzc, I suggest we refactor the runtime so it has a trait with "startup", "run" and "teardown". ActorManager will use those and that sould make it easier for crit and our bench to measure the same thing. Thoughts no code.

> AGENT

Diagnosis is right. Before committing to the refactor, a few thoughts:

**The structural fix you're describing is sound**

Splitting into `startup` / `run` / `teardown` means criterion's `iter_custom` calls only `run` against threads that are already warm — same shape goalzc has after its 3 s warmup. The criterion warmup phase (3 s of `run` calls before measurement begins) becomes useful warmup instead of being thrown away on each sample.

**Real questions before code**

- **How does `run` report counts?** Today, counts accumulate locally per-thread and surface at join. If `run` is sample-able mid-flight, we need either (a) per-actor `Arc<AtomicU64>` incremented on each dispatch — small `Relaxed` add, ~negligible at goal2/goalzc's µs scale, real at goal1's 5 ns scale; or (b) a `QueryCount` signal-and-reply round-trip — slower per `run` but no hot-path cost.
- **Probe vs no-probe split.** Today they're two separate methods. With lifecycle split, the probe choice is fixed at spawn, so it lands on `startup` — `startup_probe(...)` vs `startup_no_probe(...)`, or one fn with a flag.
- **Misuse safety.** Splitting opens "forgot teardown" / "ran before startup" failure modes. Mitigations: a `Handle` with `Drop` that shuts down if forgotten, or a typed-state pattern (`Idle → Running → Done`). Worth the cost if `RuntimeZC` is going to grow more lifecycle-aware over time.
- **Trait or just methods?** I'd defer the trait. Today there's exactly one runtime; "trait with three methods" is hypothetical-future design. Methods on `RuntimeZC` give us the same composability and we promote to a trait the day a second impl appears.

**Cheaper experiment worth running first**

Before refactoring, bump the bench's per-sample warmup from 50 ms → 500 ms (or 1 s) and drop `sample_size` from 50 → 10 to keep total bench time bounded. Same wall clock; each sample actually reaches steady state. If that closes most of the 17.5% gap, the structural cost of fresh-spawn-per-sample isn't the dominant factor and the refactor's payoff is smaller. If the gap stays wide, that's the empirical case for the lifecycle split.

The bot's read: the lifecycle refactor is worth doing eventually regardless — it's a cleaner API and future-proofs against M:N runtimes — but it's also bigger than goalzc-crit needs. The cheap experiment tells us which 0.5.0 sub-step it should land in (or whether it earns its own 0.6.0 ladder).

> DEVELOPER

I'm not concerned with benchmarks I want to get the right API and this is just part of the journey, our current code isn't any different from goal2, the one difference is zero size hence no real messages. So we've already "tested" this runtime style and I want to move forward. Make sense, no code

> AGENT

Makes sense. RuntimeZC is goal2 with `&[u8]` payload view — same threads, same mpsc, same orchestration. The runtime style was validated when goal2 landed; goalzc demonstrated the pool-backed payload variant works. Re-running cross-checks won't teach us anything new.

**What I'd do with 0.5.0-4 / 0.5.0**

Land 0.5.0-4 as-is (with the chores trimmed — drop the gap-anxiety paragraphs, keep one line noting the bench is a smoke test for the pool path, not a fidelity reference). Then close 0.5.0. Two reasons:
- The bench is cheap regression insurance against a future refactor — *does it still spin up, send, and tear down?* — even if it's not measuring what goalzc measures.
- A clean ladder close gives the lifecycle refactor a fresh `0.6.0-0` plan marker to start from. Tangling the refactor into 0.5.0 muddies the history.

**Then 0.6.0 — lifecycle refactor**

Plan-marker shape worth fleshing out before code:
- Decide methods-on-`RuntimeZC` vs trait. Recommendation: methods now, trait when a second runtime exists.
- Decide count-sampling: atomic counter vs signal-and-reply. Recommendation: `Arc<AtomicU64>` per actor — simpler API, hot-path cost is negligible at the cross-thread scale we're working at.
- Decide handle safety: bare `Handle` vs `Drop`-shutdown vs typed state. Recommendation: `Handle` with `Drop`-shutdown; teardown becomes "consume the Handle for results", and a forgotten teardown panics-on-drop or shuts down silently — pick one.
- Probe vs no-probe choice moves to `startup`.
- The bench naturally collapses: `setup` builds + warms a runtime once, `iter_custom` calls only `run(short_window)`, `teardown` at end. The 17.5% gap closes as a side effect of the right API, not as a goal.

Sound about right? If so, my next action is trimming the chores writeup, then asking again to push 0.5.0-4.

391	  - The bin's actor is in `src/bin/goalzc.rs`; binaries
392	    aren't importable as a library by other crate targets,
393	    so a copy is the cleanest path.
394	  - Five lines of duplication; the alternative

**Title**
```
feat: add goalzc-crit bench (0.5.0-4)
```

**Body**
```
Adds the criterion smoke bench for goalzc, mirroring goal2-crit's
iter_custom shape and using RuntimeZC::run_no_probe so the
measurement is probe-clean. RuntimeZC is structurally goal2 with a
swapped payload, so the runtime style is already validated by
goal2-crit; this bench is regression insurance for the pool-backed
payload path.

- crates/actor-x1/Cargo.toml: 0.5.0-3 → 0.5.0-4; new
  [[bench]] goalzc-crit entry.
- crates/actor-x1/benches/goalzc-crit.rs: new criterion bench
  (50 ms warmup + 100 ms measure per sample, fixed SIZE = 64,
  Throughput::Elements(1)).
- notes/todo.md: mark 0.5.0-3 and 0.5.0-4 done.
- notes/chores-02.md: new "## benches/goalzc-crit (0.5.0-4)"
  section (file-by-file, smoke result, design decisions).
```
